ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00036_d8e5672	The framework for the regulation of countries is very narrow. On the basis of this Constitutional Law, it is then necessary to make a simple legal framework at the federal level. In order to reach broad agreement, there are, as it were, also country competence in individual areas, particularly in procedural law. I see no problem at all because the constitutional framework is very narrow. So I do not see the danger that you now seem to have, because of your question, that there will be very different regulations.

The driver's license is gone the first time. [[General condolences.]] To the current discussion some remarks from my side. There are four important criteria that are necessary for the granting of a steering licence. These are the verification of roadworthiness, a first-aid course, an passed driver's licence test and, ultimately, a medical opinion which includes the ability to drive a car. This medical opinion has to be collected privately at the moment. With this novel, it is also possible to use military medical advice for the civil driving licence. We from the Socialists Group welcome this measure, because it is the first thing we want to do is to encourage young people and, secondly, to simplify their administration. This scheme also applies to young women who opt for a federal army career. Agenda item 3 calls on the Green Group to amend the driving licence law with a view to extending the crime catalogue for the driving licence priority system. We in the Committee have had a very lively discussion on this subject, on possible consequences, just as we had them here today in this debate. At present, catalogue 13 shows serious traffic violations, which, in addition to a fine, also lead to entries in the driving licence priority system. What does that mean? – The Federal Minister has already raised a number of points: After such an entry, one is in principle a risk driver, in the case of a second offence training and behavioural measures are provided, and in the case of a third offence, the driver's licence is issued to the Authority for three months. The basic strategy of this crime catalogue is to include dangerous elements with a particularly high risk potential, and, ladies and gentlemen, this high risk potential has not been seen by the majority in the Committee. Transport safety is a common responsibility, and everyone has to contribute to it. Studies show that carelessness and distraction are the most common causes of accidents. Therefore, awareness actions such as the action currently carried out by the ASFINAG should be fully supported. We insist on raising awareness of penalties, and our group supports all the initiatives that pursue this objective. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister.

On the matter of care itself, so that we have already made this clear here, Mr Doppler: Where we pay a promotion, people are registered, because without registration there is no promotion. We currently have 45,318 persons in charge of the SVA, which are active. We have once again 15 000 commercial rights which rest at an hour, when we calculate everything together, and 45 000 are active. We're only paying off if that's someone who's registered, and we're currently paying out this 24-hour funding to 18 000 people affected. With regard to those who are only cared for by family members at home – in order to bring you closer to the latest figures here too – we are currently at around 200 000. These are only cared for by relatives who do not buy anything – at least temporarily, they do not buy anything, because it can change the situation. 145 000 are given mobile care, 72,000 are given hospital care. 15 000 people live in so-called alternative forms of residence or in part-station facilities. That is to say, we have tried, and for this target group, coming from these 200 000, we have given answers. We have given the answer of family social security if they are still in working age but get out to care for them. This works, 10,000 are currently taking advantage of it. It is clear, however, that many of the members of the family are already at an age that is no longer workable. I think we have taken a right step with the care career, a right step with the care part-time, where I do not hide and say openly: The care career is running, the care part time is not running. That has many backgrounds, but it is de facto so: If so, then you take care of the care. As a result, I believe that we are also well placed in what we are doing in preparation for training issues. All of them are more or less required to expand training accordingly. We ourselves, of course, also benefit from the time of the unemployment of those concerned and try to develop and enable at least 4 000 to 5 000 unemployed people each year in training, either caregivers or even in part, health care diplomas. That means the topic is, I think, not negated. The subject is not going to fall asleep, but it is an issue in which we know the development very, very well and precisely. However, it is also a matter on which we know very well and precisely how we simply have to address the challenges of the future. Otherwise, the employees of the Service Department of the Ministry of Social Affairs are subject to exactly the same data protection rules as is already in place in the Interior. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00036_d8e14575	Mr Stöger spoke on a point of view. - Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00036_d8e14596	Mr President. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen in the House! Mr Jannach. Thank you, first of all, for the request, second also for the praise you give me, that I have also answered it. It is very important to me to show the Austrian population as well: What is the responsibility of the Federal Ministry of Health? . We are responsible for veterinary import regulations. Why? – Because, of course, we want to ensure and ensure that, when pigmeat is introduced to Austria, we comply with European rules. This is about preventing the introduction of diseases, of animal diseases, of zoonoses, and we are also concerned that we comply with animal welfare standards as a whole. In Austria, food, especially meat, pigmeat, is being placed on the market, which is produced properly. I can't help how statistics from Statistics Austria are made. That's where you need to ask the competent authority. From my point of view, it is very crucial how the control situation in the field of veterinary medicine is regulated. How does the official system look? . I am now also saying this to all Members for information: We have a control system, the TRACES – so TRAde Control and Expert System. This is a single central database in the European Union. Any meat that is or is brought out of the European Union shall be entered there. This entry means that this meat is also controlled and verified by veterinary experts. We have in Austria a facility that controls directly in Austria, this is located at the airport in Linz. Veterinary products can be transported there to Austria. If they come across other countries of the European Union, they will be registered in this system. As far as imports from third countries are concerned, you have correctly said that there are only three third countries which are allowed to supply meat to Austria, namely Canada, Chile and the USA. This concerns live animals, but also pigmeat. No pigmeat can be imported into Austria from other countries. And even if the answer is correct, it's back-imports: Of course, Austrian producers are allowed to deliver to any country, if the country also accepts it. In practice, however, reciprocity is always shown. We sometimes deliver meat to other countries outside the European Union, which are not Chile, Canada and the USA. When the statistics speak of imports from Austria, they mean imports from outside the borders of Austria. When we talk about imports, we are talking about what is being spent outside the European Union. And that makes a difference. All food of animal origin shall be recorded and presented in this official European control system. The Austrian system of control works. How do you recognize that? – This is recognised by the fact that veterinary controls are taking place and that we have managed to be disease-free. We have managed to be free of disease, Austria has a very special position on the market. We – and that was a great concern to me: more clarity, and consumers should know that too – enforced the designation of origin at European level in a power act. That was a difficult undertaking. It will be at the European level from 1. Give a mandatory designation of origin for fresh, chilled and frozen pigmeat on April 2015. This is also extended to sheep, goat and poultry meat, and there must be awarded where the animal has been fattened and slaughtered. For example, if one praises a special quality, such as Austria as a country of origin, then the animal must also be born in that country. If the AMA seal praises Austrian goods, it must be ensured that the animal is born in Austria and that it has also been fashioned and slaughtered in Austria. In this sense, the veterinary authorities have a positive effect, the protection system works. I protect the health of the Austrians, I protect the health of the people who live in Austria, and this award model has proved successful.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00036_d8e15866	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The Austrian social security system is a compulsory insurance system. In other words, in the event of the performance of a certain activity, the compulsory insurance shall take place ex legibly, regardless of whether that is intended by the insured person, whether he considers it useful or necessary. Therefore, two or more social insurance-related employment relationships lead to a so-called multi-insurance and therefore, of course, to a multiple contribution obligation. In principle, all income from work should therefore be taken into account in the calculation of contributions. However, the legislator aims to ensure that all contributions are made only up to the maximum contribution basis. In the event of a breach of the maximum basis of contribution, a refund of the contributions shall therefore be made or the multiple insured person shall apply for the so-called differential requirement, according to which the basic amount of the contribution shall be determined in advance in such a way as not to exceed the maximum basis of contribution. [[Abg. Schwentner: And for what?]] These rules apply in health and pension insurance, but not in accident insurance. There has been unemployment insurance since 1. The possibility of reimbursement of contributions was granted to the employee in January 2005. However, it should also not be ignored that the multiple insurance has, of course, also benefit-related consequences and that this really involves important aspects of performance law. For example, in health insurance benefits can only be granted once, cash benefits, i.e. sickness benefit and weekly benefit, but are very likely to be paid several times. However, the current question relates only to the contribution aspects of multi-insurance and, above all, to the meeting of self-employed and self-employed work, leaving many phenomena associated with multi-insurance quite simply disregarded. For example, if a person has two or more of the employment relationships covered by the ASVG, the service provider shall pay the social security contributions. However, only the employee, and not the service provider, has the possibility of reimbursement in sickness, pension and unemployment insurance. The service providers' contributions thus remain in social insurance. Another particular feature of a multiple ASVG-compulsory insurance is that an application for differential prescription is not possible. In other words, the basis of contributions cannot be determined on request in such a way as not to exceed the maximum basis of contribution. A special feature is also normized in § 26 GSVG. According to this provision, in the case of a GSVG-compulsory insured person who is also engaged in an activity under the ASVG, the contribution basis shall be calculated on the basis of the ASVG on the minimum contribution basis in accordance with the GSVG. Therefore, if a trade licence holder achieves losses from his GSVG activity, he shall not, in principle, be liable to the GSVG contributions to be paid on the basis of the minimum contribution basis if the ASVG contribution base already reaches the GSVG minimum contribution basis. So you see, ladies and gentlemen, how complex the rules in the field of multi-insurance are. The fact that the combination of social security funds in the present question cannot solve this problem alone can already be seen by the fact that the situation in several self-employed activities following the ASVG is no easier than if self-employed and self-employed work coincides. But one thing is clear: Simplification would be more than appropriate. However, other areas of problem should also be considered in this context. One of the causes of the increase in multiple insurance is the involvement of the so-called atypical employees in social security. Especially in the case of atypical employment conditions, the “joint examination of pay-related taxes” often leads to a requalification, for example from work contracts to service contracts. The consequences associated with this are partly threat to existence for the employee. In addition, after the examination has ended, the separate legal remedy is an essential criticism. The other differences between social security law and tax law do not necessarily make it easier for auditors to do so. In the interests of simplification in the field of social security law, a comprehensive reform is needed, which should in particular include harmonisation between social security law and tax law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00036_d8e17743	Mr President. Mr. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, Dear ladies and gentlemen on the gallery and in front of the screens! The report on the situation of youth employment and apprenticeship training in Austria 2012–2013 is thoughtful for me. The decline in apprenticeships has been around 1 000 annually since 2009 and has even increased to 1,500 in 2013 compared to the previous year. The Federal Government responded to this situation with the funding of apprenticeships for entrepreneurs. However, this job promotion does not bring the desired success, despite a budget commitment of 650 million. The reason for this is that SMEs are no longer able to afford apprenticeship training despite operating support. With the integrative vocational training, the Federal Government responded to young people who are unable to create a normal teaching or are unable to complete it in the specified time. The sum of these employees is 6 000 apprentices with inclusive vocational training, of which 76 percent are in the extended training period, and 24 percent make part-qualification. The problem with this model is that five years after IBA, about 50 percent of apprentices are in no corresponding employment. With the over-company The Federal Government is responding to the teaching scene crisis – a collection network for unemployed young people seeking teaching places. The disadvantage is a high drop-out quota. With the modularization of apprenticeship training, the Federal Government is responding to the original objective of adjustment of the apprenticeship landscape. The advantage of this variant is a flexible training possibility also for occupations with low apprenticeships. The disadvantage of this variant is high cost for special modules. The teaching with Matura is an essential upgrading of dual vocational training. Currently about 11,000 apprentices complete this teaching with Matura. The range between teaching places and job seekers opens further and further. In the tourism and leisure industry there is an overhang of around 900 teaching places and the highest drop-out quota after the over-company teaching workshops. Youths with migration backgrounds present the statistics in the drop-out quota, as well as in the non-filed course examination, or most often leave the education system after fulfilling the compulsory schooling. From my point of view, all the measures described above have only brought modest success in the fight for youth employment and apprenticeship training in Austria. The reason for this is not the unreasonable effort of the Federal Government and the lack of resources, but the fact that SMEs have neither the time nor the money for appropriate apprenticeship training. My proposal to improve the current situation is as follows: to evaluate the measures already introduced and discussed, to force tourism and the leisure industry and to capitalise SMEs on the instruments of economic promotion. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00036_d8e18562	Mr President. Dear ladies and gentlemen! Our democratically important and also architecturally valuable Parliament building has undoubtedly come into existence, and there is a need for urgent general reorganisation. Over the past few months, many experts have succeeded in dealing with this building with a lot of expertise and experience, and also in knowing it, as well as many advocates – to whom I also count – with a lot of patience and persuasion – but at the head of these advocates, Madam President Prammer, I am sure, was able to work out a concept for a sustainable renovation of this Parliament building in lengthy discussions. This concept focuses on several points: For the first time, we have succeeded in deciding on the most economic and also appropriate rehabilitation option in the long term. This requires enormous preparatory work, which the experts, as I said, thanked for. We will be able to carry out a professional implementation of this non-complicated project with the experienced professionals of the Bundesimmobiliengesellschaft. It will ensure that users, i.e. all of us, as well as the employees of the house, will be involved in a useful form, namely, on the one hand in a building committee and on the other hand in a user advisory board. And this law, which we will adopt today, also ensures that the cost of this project is limited. I would like to thank all those who have contributed to this. On the one hand, these are the employees and the experts of the Parliamentary Directorate, and on the other I thank the external experts, consultants and authorities for professional support and support for this project. I would like to thank all those who are politically responsible at the Presidential Conference, but also those who have agreed to participate in this project outside of the Commissions to the jury for the choice of architects. I would like to thank, above all, the constructive cooperation, the willingness of all six political groups to take responsibility for this project and to refrain from wanting to make a cheap point here in the populist sense, in the demagogic sense. No, everyone was willing to take responsibility for this project. The result, ladies and gentlemen, is an all-party proposal that we can present today and, hopefully, decide. In my capacity as President-in-Office of the Council, I will immediately take the next steps provided for in the law following the legal decision-making process. That is to say, the creation of the realization society together with the Bundesimmobiliengesellschaft, the establishment of the Building Committee and the User Advisory Council, including the completion of the architectural selection and negotiation process. A brief comment might also be made: It was wrong yesterday in the “Report” by the ORF that there is already a winner of the architectural competition or negotiation process. Rather, it is true that a jury of projects presented by them, including unknown architectural offices, has assessed and arranged projects. I do not know the names of all the projects presented today, and those responsible are currently holding negotiations with all candidates, as foreseen in a negotiation procedure. There may or may change somewhat in the order of these conversations. In any case, contrary to what was claimed yesterday in the ORF, the winner is of course not yet certain. This will be the case in the early autumn when this negotiation procedure is concluded – and not before. And one last thing: Only then, when the winner is determined and the award to the architectural office has been made can the gross and detailed planning be developed, the necessary tenders are made and, I hope, then, in 2017 after we have moved out, will the construction work actually begin. This is the plan, and I shall therefore try to take the next steps. Ladies and gentlemen! With this rehabilitation concept, we guarantee the Austrian Republic and thus Austrians the material and ideal value of federal property, and we also ensure for our work – it is not about us as individuals, but about the parliamentary democracy of this country – a dignified and appropriate framework. I therefore ask all of you to give your consent to this bill so that we can then take the next steps I have described before. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00036_d8e18682	Mr President. Minister! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! Considering that Parliament was established between 1871 and 1883 and that only two renovations have been made since its construction, namely 1956, the reconstruction of several bombs and 2005 the renovation of the ramp, which was difficult to build, then it is recognized how urgently it is necessary to introduce the Parliament building of a renovation. There are serious flaws in the area of fire protection as regards evacuation, as far as accessibility is concerned, but also in terms of building resistance, house technology, glass roofs and roof constructions, all these areas. The building has long exceeded its normal service life in many areas. For this reason alone, it is necessary to carry out a renovation and to restore a law-compliant state of this house. ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00036_d8e18842	Mr President. Minister! High house! Ladies and gentlemen! We from the Stronach team welcome today's adoption of the Parliament Building Rehabilitation Act. The Parliament building is not just a symbol of the Republic and democracy, it is part of the ensemble of the Ringstraße and therefore of urban relevance, a historic building under monument protection. Theophil Hansen created one of his most important works here, and after World War II, the architects Max Fellerer and Eugen Wörle, when a rapid reconstruction of the destroyed substance was necessary, left their design manuscript here. This too is a sign of the new beginning of the Second Republic. It is therefore a great responsibility to comply with both the historical substance and the political symbolism and to carry out the reorganisation as carefully as possible. We of the Stronach team are aware of our responsibility and have therefore agreed to a sustainable renovation. This is a decision taken by all political groups, as the President-in-Office has already said, because political considerations have no place in such a far-reaching question. Instead, we need to work together to find the best solution in terms of usability and rehabilitation, because Parliament must be a modern job for Members and enable the complex parliamentary work to be carried out efficiently. MEPs from all parliamentary clubs were also involved in bringing in precisely these aspects from the perspective of practical work in Parliamentary operations. This law also limits costs, and we also bear responsibility for future generations. Today, we decide on a maximum cost limit for both the renovation and the interim location and for the relocation. In all, this is an important step for the future of our Parliament. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00036_d8e19944	Mr President. Mr. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen in the High House! These are requests concerning the use of private security services, forced measures and accommodation in net beds in psychiatric hospitals. The history: The Public Prosecutor's Office has examined about 63 psychiatric hospitals and has found that private security services continue to take over activities reserved exclusively for the care staff, for which care staff are trained. Why is that so? Forced measures are always an intervention in personal rights and may in principle only be applied in the event of serious self- and foreign threats, and of course only by trained care staff. This is also very well secured by law. This is a high-quality measure, which is precisely regulated by the Health and Hospital Care Act and reserved for doctors and nurses. The claim would be that all those who work in this field are specifically trained in acute psychiatry, and that escalations, conflicts, conflict moods can already be seen in advance. For this, there should be a level plan for de-escalation and de-escalation measures should be put in place: in advance, to secure privacy, to create retreats and to offer a personal, familiar, professional relationship. And it is only when all therapeutic measures are really exhausted that it may be necessary, unfortunately, to fix people or to isolate them or also to give them medication against their will. How often and how often such a thing happens is simply and simply a quality criterion for psychiatric departments. And we see that there are very big differences here – often in the same hospital. Because the departments are run differently, forced action is more common or less common. A crucial point in this is the lack of personnel. I believe that all those who have worked in this area know that conflict potential, conflict tension, especially when there are conflicts in the team and when the care staff are insecure. And, of course, care staff are uncertain when it is left alone in difficult situations. They have all rejected these requests on the grounds that it would be all well regulated in the Health and Hospital Care Act anyway. Yes, it may be well regulated, but it does happen, and it seems that private security services do or participate in care services tasks. I mean, we need clear restrictions here. I was – I have to say – horrified at the discussion in the Health Committee. I think it was a very unqualified discussion, which is very close to inhumane statements and to basic human attitudes. [[Abg. Belakovich-Jenewein: Come to the Committee!]] I think it must be clear to all of us that there is an urgent need to do something here. To that end, I would ask the Federal Minister to remove the issues in this grey zone by decree. It is intended to identify the tasks and the cooperation in the implementation of compulsory measures of private security services. Any patient activity must be documented exactly. In other words, there must be a documentation obligation, and at the request of the Public Prosecutor's Office, this documentation must also be given an insight into it. [[Federal Minister Stöger: This is the legal situation!]] There should also be a register for the collection of restrictions on freedom. In this case, there was an unfair attack on the part of Mr Spindelberger, who said that this would come close to the mental health card of the past. Of course, that's not meant. It is an anonymized central recording of forced measures meant as a feedback instrument for the institutions that, compared to others, are to have a quality assurance instrument and thus have the opportunity to reflect and discuss why forced measures occur less often in one institution and in the other less frequently. Last point: The European Committee on the Prevention of Torture and Inhuman or Humiliating Treatment or Punishment has already advised Austria in 2009 and after: Net beds and cage-like shelters for mentally ill people, for people with mental impairments and for people with disabilities should be stopped. I would ask you, Minister, to support these concerns. I am personally concerned about these issues, and I am sure I will stay on them.

The information campaign, the possibilities for debate and discussion with the European institutions, i.e. the Council, the Commission and Parliament, must therefore be improved. We appreciate it when the MEPs come to the main committee or to the EU subcommittee and participate in the discussions there. However, we must be careful that we do not shoot beyond the goal in good intention. If we look at the proposal, we see that the MEPs of Europe would be given more right to speak than any Member here in this House, with the exception of the President. I do not think that can be the aim of the request. I would therefore like to propose the following: At present there are discussions and negotiations on a comprehensive reform package, in which we have the EU procedures can be improved. In these discussions, we should include, discuss and deal with all the different ideas that exist. Some time ago, we had a first specially established exchange between the Members of the European Parliament and the National Council. A repetition was desired. This is also one of the proposals you hear. Let us therefore continue to discuss these ideas, which are on the table, in the course of the reform pact. - Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00036_d8e23473	Next speaker: Mrs Dr. Karl. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00036_d8e23494	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Of course, the intention to make decision-making in the EU more transparent and comprehensible for the population and thereby contribute to greater transparency and objectivity in discussions on EU issues should be supported. In addition, European issues are becoming increasingly important, that EU issues also become more important to our parliamentary activity. I could personally imagine that there is an opportunity for the Members of the European Union to speak in the negotiations on those agenda items on the participation of the National Council in European legislation. However, I am right for my previous speaker to say that there is no need to shoot beyond the goal here, and that we must therefore look at the very limits of such a speech. I am convinced that there will be a lively and detailed debate in the Committee on the Rules of Procedure, and I believe that there should be absolutely no such debate. - Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00036_d8e23525	Last word sign: Mr Brosz. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-09-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00036_d8e23546	It is not so mysterious as to what we have proposed, even then, when it came to the reform of the Rules of Procedure in relation to Europe. Our idea has always been that – and it is the question of how to put it exactly – there should be a right of speech on the subject of materials relating to Europe. I believe that the present proposal would not really do well with a general right of speech, because it would be quite clear that this right of speech would only be used in this form to a very limited extent. We know that the national Council meetings and the meetings in the de facto European Parliament take place at the same time that it would be extremely difficult for European parliamentarians to participate in this rhythm. And if you want to do it in such a way that it really is supposed to be lively, then you have to take it into consideration. However, this is only useful if times are chosen in which this is also possible. It would also be exciting, in my view, to make an exchange, in committees or in a mixed committee, of regular exchanges. So our intention is: Yes to a right of speech, yes to a reference to European matter. And all the way. It is important to make it so that it is also taken into account when planning the meetings. Now we all know from the political groups that the European Parliamentarians have major problems in really participating in it. They are sitting de facto in Europe all the time. At the time when we have our meetings, they are supposed to be there, so there are also difficulties with them. So if it is to be viable, there must also be the structural possibility of this participation. And a general right of speech would more likely result in saying after a year: So, now you've created the right to speak, why were they never there? This should be spared, because it will be quite difficult to live up to expectations. Therefore, realistic expectations and realistic implementation.
